{"content": "     1|#!/usr/bin/env python3\n     2|\"\"\"Score podcast episodes with OpenRouter Qwen using compact DJ schema.\"\"\"\n     3|import argparse, json, os, re, sqlite3, time, html\n     4|from pathlib import Path\n     5|from datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta\n     6|from openrouter_spend import openrouter_post_json\n     7|\n     8|BASE = Path('/opt/data/podcast_digest')\n     9|DB = BASE / 'episodes.sqlite'\n    10|OUTDIR = BASE / 'outputs'\n    11|MODEL = os.getenv('PODCAST_OSS_MODEL', 'qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b')\n    12|BATCH_SIZE = int(os.getenv('PODCAST_SCORE_BATCH_SIZE', '10'))\n    13|\n    14|\n    15|def gateway_env_key(name='OPENROUTER_API_KEY'):\n    16|    # Terminal subprocesses can have stale env after Railway redeploy; gateway PID 1 has current env.\n    17|    val = os.getenv(name)\n    18|    if val:\n    19|        return val\n    20|    try:\n    21|        raw = Path('/proc/1/environ').read_bytes().split(b'\\0')\n    22|        prefix = (name + '=').encode()\n    23|        for item in raw:\n    24|            if item.startswith(prefix):\n    25|                return item[len(prefix):].decode()\n    26|    except Exception:\n    27|        pass\n    28|    return None\n    29|\n    30|\n    31|def clean(s, n=1200):\n    32|    if not s:\n    33|        return ''\n    34|    s = re.sub(r'<[^>]+>', ' ', str(s))\n    35|    s = html.unescape(s)\n    36|    s = re.sub(r'\\s+', ' ', s).strip()\n    37|    return s[:n]\n    38|\n    39|\n    40|def load_episodes(since_hours=None):\n    41|    con = sqlite3.connect(DB)\n    42|    con.row_factory = sqlite3.Row\n    43|    params = []\n    44|    where = ''\n    45|    if since_hours is not None:\n    46|        since = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=float(since_hours))).isoformat()\n    47|        where = 'where published >= ?'\n    48|        params.append(since)\n    49|    rows = con.execute(f'''\n    50|        select id, show_name, ring, priority, title, link, published, summary, duration, score as old_score, label as old_label\n    51|        from episodes\n    52|        {where}\n    53|        order by published desc, show_name, title\n    54|    ''', params).fetchall()\n    55|    return [dict(r) for r in rows]\n    56|\n    57|\n    58|def call_openrouter(api_key, batch, attempt=1):\n    59|    system = \"\"\"You are DJ Mauch's podcast episode filter. Score EPISODES, not shows. Shows are only source priors.\n    60|DJ wants a daily text digest that casts a wider net summarizing what was said, while the weekly audio podcast-of-podcasts is finely tuned.\n    61|Favor episodes likely to provide: market read, capital allocation implication, CEO/operator insight, AI platform/business model insight, durable framework, or personal/professional toolkit addition.\n    62|Do not over-reward generic AI keywords, news recaps, politics, celebrity, generic VC promo, or shallow founder stories.\n    63|\n    64|DJ calibration from the first 100-episode pass:\n    65|- Be stricter with `listen`; reserve it for original audio likely worth DJ's time. Daily can be broad, but listen should be sparse.\n    66|- `digest` means worth summarizing in text; many good technical/business episodes belong here, not listen.\n    67|- Promote: Benedict Evans appearances/rational AI economics, Mercor CEO on AI labor/model economics, Dara/Uber AV strategy, Gita Gopinath global rates, Ranjan Roy on AI boom warnings, Toast business breakdown, Stratechery best-of content.\n    68|- Summarize but do not necessarily listen: enterprise data infrastructure/Fivetran agents, Axiom Math, operator-led PE, RenMac market models, Mark Pincus product frameworks, evolutionary AI models, personal AI workflows, Terry Sejnowski, China vs Nvidia.\n    69|- Penalize/skip even if AI-heavy: Exa search infra promo, overly technical continual learning, video/generative-media agent demos, generic AI consciousness/alarmism, vertical vendor AI stories (Aircall/Mitel/elder care), Corgi culture promo, Elon/media narrative, consumer brand playbooks, crypto chatter, generic Bloomberg/news recaps.\n    70|- Low-priority shows can surface if the episode is right: Vergecast Nvidia chip war and MFM Idiot Index were interesting enough to learn more.\n    71|Return ONLY valid JSON with key results: an array of objects. {"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Use exactly three states when you do alert:**\n  1. **OK** — everything is fine; no DJ action.\n  2. **Warning** — something is off but not broken; say what is wrong and what DJ needs to do.\n  3. **Broken** — something is broken; say what is broken and what DJ needs to do now.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what exact action does DJ need to take?* If the action is for Hermes, say `DJ action: none. Hermes should ...` explicitly.\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n## Report jobs: daily + weekly, with truthful zeroes\n\nSpend-style report jobs are not health alerts. They should be designed as **explicit daily and weekly summaries** with stable cadence and unambiguous windows.\n\nRules:\n- **Daily and weekly should both exist** when the user asks for both. Do not assume a daily job covers the weekly need.\n- **Report the actual ledger result, even if it is zero.** If spend is `0.0000` or the pricing source is `included`, say that plainly instead of implying failure or activity.\n- **Do not infer work happened from the existence of a scheduled run.** A successful cron wakeup is not evidence that any spend events occurred.\n- **Call out pricing caveats explicitly.** If `estimated_cost_usd` is zero because the provider or pricing path is still unresolved, the report should surface that as a caveat, not hide it.\n- **Use the same source of truth for both cadence variants** so the daily and weekly numbers reconcile (same ledger, different window).\n- **If a report belongs with a health check, prefer a single combined daily wrapper over two separate Telegram messages.** The wrapper should run the health probe first, then the report, and send one scan-friendly message when healthy. Keep the dedicated report job only if it serves a different audience or cadence.\n\nFor spend reports, the primary question is: *what did the ledger record in the window?* Not: *did the job run?*\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n## LLM-backed cron jobs: use the shared accounting helper\n\nWhen a cron job calls OpenRouter directly from a script, prefer the shared helper pattern used by Hermes-owned scripts instead of hand-rolling `requests.post(...)` plus ad hoc usage logging.\n\nGuideline:\n- centralize request + spend recording in a shared helper;\n- attach stable metadata like `project_slug`, `source`, `platform`, and a stage name;\n- verify the rollout by compiling the changed scripts and searching for lingering direct OpenRouter URL calls in the active script tree.\n\nThis keeps token tracking, cost estimation, and ledger attribution aligned across future projects.\n\n## Spend reports: use the session insights path, not the raw ledger\n\nFor Hermes daily/weekly spend reporting, the authoritative reporting source is the *session insights* path (`SessionDB` + `InsightsEngine`), not the narrower `agent.spend_ledger` table alone.\n\nWhy:\n- the raw ledger can show only a partial view of spend attribution;\n- `InsightsEngine.generate(days=...)` already exposes `estimated_cost` and `actual_cost` plus session/model/platform breakdowns;\n- daily and weekly reports should answer: ### assistant

```text
You were right to push back. I found the actual failure, fixed it, and regenerated the digest.

## What was broken
The collector was relying on `feedparser` dates, but several fresh feeds were only exposing `pubDate` in the raw RSS. That meant new episodes were being saved with blank `published` timestamps, so the 24h window looked empty even when it wasn’t.

## What I fixed
- Added a **raw RSS pubDate fallback**
- Added **RFC 2822 date parsing**
- Re-ran the collector
- Re-ran the full daily pipeline

## Verified result
- New episodes now found in the 24h window: **11**
- Final digest generated successfully at:

`/opt/data/podcast_digest/outputs/2026-06-10_1048-daily-podcast-digest-24h.md`

## Current calibrated ranking
Here’s the current top-of-list output, ranked by the scoring pass:

1. **Jensen Huang / Training Data** — score **90** — `listen`
   - *LIVE: Jensen Huang on Building the Dynamo of the Intelligence Age*
   - Why: foundational AI infrastructure / energy / stack framing

2. **Stratechery** — score **85** — `digest`
   - *Fable 5, Anthropic Alignment, AI Tiers*
   - Why: durable framework on model tiers, governance, and alignment

3. **The Cognitive Revolution** — score **82** — `digest`
   - *Babysitting the Machine: Glean’s Rebecca Hinds on the Hidden Human Labor of AI at Work*
   - Why: enterprise AI adoption friction and hidden labor

4. **All-In Podcast** — score **80** — `digest`
   - *Bill Maris: How Google Could Crush AI Competitors, Why Small Funds Win, and AI’s Atari Stage*
   - Why: AI platform strategy + VC timing + market structure

5. **a16z Podcast** — score **78** — `digest`
   - *AI, Growth, and the Future of Healthcare*
   - Why: AI + enterprise transformation in a regulated sector

6. **The Tim Ferriss Show** — score **75** — `digest`
   - *Max Levchin, PayPal and Affirm*
   - Why: founder/operator lessons and capital allocation context

7. **All-In Podcast** — score **70** — `digest`
   - *Dan Dreyfus: America’s Critical Minerals Crisis is Here*
   - Why: AI infrastructure supply chain implications

8. **Bloomberg Surveillance — US Economic Outlook and Bond Signals** — score **65** — `scan`
   - Why: useful macro context, but less directly strategic

9. **The Vergecast — How Steve Jobs became Steve Jobs** — score **60** — `scan`

10. **The Prof G Pod — Raging Moderates + Bezos taxes** — score **50** — `scan`

11. **Bloomberg Surveillance TV** — score **45** — `skip`

## Bottom line
The digest is now working and the ranking is no longer falsely empty.

If you want, I can do the next calibration pass now and tighten the ranking so it better matches *your* taste — for example:
- make it more aggressive about **operator/CEO insight**
- down-rank **macro news / policy commentary**
- make **AI infrastructure + platform strategy** rise to the top even more consistently
```

Himalaya CLI installed (`himalaya --version` to verify)\n2. A configuration file at `~/.config/himalaya/config.toml`\n3. IMAP/SMTP credentials configured (password stored securely)\n\n### Installation\n\n``​`bash\n# Pre-built binary (Linux/macOS — recommended)\ncurl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pimalaya/himalaya/master/install.sh | PREFIX=~/.local sh\n\n# macOS via Homebrew\nbrew install himalaya\n\n# Or via cargo (any platform with Rust)\ncargo install himalaya --locked\n``​`\n\n## Configuration Setup\n\nRun the interactive wizard to set up an account:\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya account configure\n``​`\n\nOr create `~/.config/himalaya/config.toml` manually:\n\n``​`toml\n[accounts.personal]\nemail = \"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\"\ndisplay-name = \"Your Name\"\ndefault = true\n\nbackend.type = \"imap\"\nbackend.host = \"imap.example.com\"\nbackend.port = 993\nbackend.encryption.type = \"tls\"\nbackend.login = \"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\"\nbackend.auth.type = \"password\"\nbackend.auth.cmd = \"pass show email/imap\"  # or use keyring\n\nmessage.send.backend.type = \"smtp\"\nmessage.send.backend.host = \"smtp.example.com\"\nmessage.send.backend.port = 587\nmessage.send.backend.encryption.type = \"start-tls\"\nmessage.send.backend.login = \"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\"\nmessage.send.backend.auth.type = \"password\"\nmessage.send.backend.auth.cmd = \"pass show email/smtp\"\n\n# Folder aliases (himalaya v1.2.0+ syntax). Required whenever the\n# server's folder names don't match himalaya's canonical names\n# (inbox/sent/drafts/trash). Gmail is the common case — see\n# `references/configuration.md` for the `[Gmail]/Sent Mail` mapping.\nfolder.aliases.inbox = \"INBOX\"\nfolder.aliases.sent = \"Sent\"\nfolder.aliases.drafts = \"Drafts\"\nfolder.aliases.trash = \"Trash\"\n``​`\n\n> **Heads up on the alias syntax.** Pre-v1.2.0 docs used a\n> `[accounts.NAME.folder.alias]` sub-section (singular `alias`).\n> v1.2.0 silently ignores that form — TOML parses fine, but the\n> alias resolver never reads it, so every lookup falls through to\n> the canonical name. On Gmail this means save-to-Sent fails *after*\n> SMTP delivery succeeds, and `himalaya message send` exits non-zero.\n> Any caller (agent, script, user) that retries on that exit code\n> will re-run the entire send — including SMTP — producing duplicate\n> emails to recipients. Always use `folder.aliases.X` (plural, dotted\n> keys, directly under `[accounts.NAME]`).\n\n## Hermes Integration Notes\n\n- **Reading, listing, searching, moving, deleting** all work directly through the terminal tool\n- **Composing/replying/forwarding** — piped input (`cat << EOF | himalaya template send`) is recommended for reliability. Interactive `$EDITOR` mode works with `pty=true` + background + process tool, but requires knowing the editor and its commands\n- Use `--output json` for structured output that's easier to parse programmatically\n- The `himalaya account configure` wizard requires interactive input — use PTY mode: `terminal(command=\"himalaya account configure\", pty=true)`\n\n## Common Operations\n\n### List Folders\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya folder list\n``​`\n\n### List Emails\n\nList emails in INBOX (default):\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya envelope list\n``​`\n\nList emails in a specific folder:\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya envelope list --folder \"Sent\"\n``​`\n\nList with pagination:\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya envelope list --page 1 --page-size 20\n``​`\n\n### Search Emails\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya envelope list from [REDACTED_EMAIL] subject meeting\n``​`\n\n### Read an Email\n\nRead email by ID (shows plain text):\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya message read 42\n``​`\n\nExport raw MIME:\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya message export 42 --full\n``​`\n\n### Reply to an Email\n\nTo reply non-interactively from Hermes, read the original message, compose a reply, and pipe it:\n\n``​`bash\n# Get the reply template, edit it, and send\nhimalaya template reply 42 | sed 's/^$/\\nYour reply text here\\n/' | himalaya template send\n``​`\n\nOr build the reply manually:\n\n``​`bash\ncat << 'EOF' | himalaya template send\nFrom: [REDACTED_EMAIL]\nTo: [REDACTED_EMAIL]\nSubject: Re: Original Subject\nIn-Reply-To: <original-message-id>\n\nYour reply here.\nEOF\n``​`\n\nReply-all (interactive — needs $EDITOR, use template approach above instead):\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya message reply 42 --all\n``​`\n\n### Forward an Email\n\n``​`bash\n# Get forward template and pipe with modifications\nhimalaya template forward 42 | sed 's/^To:.*/To: [REDACTED_EMAIL]/' | himalaya template send\n``​`\n\n### Write a New Email\n\n**Non-interactive (use this from Hermes)** — pipe the message via stdin:\n\n``​`bash\ncat << 'EOF' | himalaya template send\nFrom: [REDACTED_EMAIL]\nTo: [REDACTED_EMAIL]\nSubject: Test Message\n\nHello from Himalaya!\nEOF\n``​`\n\nOr with headers flag:\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya message write -H \"To:[REDACTED_EMAIL]\" -H \"Subject:Test\" \"Message body here\"\n``​`\n\nNote: `himalaya message write` without piped input opens `$EDITOR`. This works with `pty=true` + background mode, but piping is simpler and more reliable.\n\n### Move/Copy Emails\n\nMove to folder:\n\n``​`bash\nhimalaya message move 42 \"Ar
